Now I just don’t feel much about them.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt sad, empty, or down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say most of the day nearly every day. I’ve had some down stretches, but I haven’t really been keeping track that closely.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your sleep has changed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it has. For a while I was only sleeping like three or four hours and still somehow showing up wired for work and class. But now it’s more like I can’t fall asleep, or I wake up before my alarm and feel like I didn’t rest at all.\n\nDoctor: During that time when you were sleeping only three or four hours, did you still feel unusually energetic?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I felt kind of charged up, like I couldn’t really sit still. Even after a full day working on pipes, I’d still go to my evening class, then come home and reorganize all my tools, clean the apartment, or start drawing out systems instead of just relaxing.\n\nDoctor: During that time when you were sleeping only three or four hours, did you feel unusually confident or like you could handle more than other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I did. I felt like I knew better than the instructors and even the senior guys at work. Like I could design a better system than them, and if people would just listen to me, I could change how buildings in Miami handled water. Looking back, that sounds like a lot, but at the time it felt true.\n\nDoctor: During that wired period, were you talking more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I was. In class or at the shop, I’d start explaining some idea and then just keep going. I’d talk over people sometimes and barely stop to breathe, even when I could tell they were trying to move on or get back to work.\n\nDoctor: During that wired period, did you do anything unusually risky or impulsive for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I mean, I was more intense and doing more than usual, but I can’t think of anything actually risky or impulsive like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
